Many such compounds are not mutagenic. The presence of sulfonate may be relatively inert. However, dimethyl sulfate is mutagenic due to alkylation. But dimethyl sulfate has two methyl groups attached to sulfur. This compound has one O-alkyl and one methyl group. It's more like a sulfate ester with one methyl on S and one alkyl group. Dimethyl sulfate is more potent alkylating agent. This may be less reactive but could still alkylate DNA. I'd lean that it's likely mutagenic. The dataset maybe indicates such group leads to mutagenicity. So choose B.

Thus answer: B.<|end|><|start|>assistant<|channel|>final<|message|>Answer: (B)<|return|>

Context: Membrane permeability is a measure of the ability of a drug to pass through a cellular membrane through passive diffusion, which is necessary for the drug to affect a cell. In general, small hydrophobic drugs can easily pass through membranes whereas large polar/charged drugs do not easily pass through. PAMPA (parallel artificial membrane permeability assay) is a commonly employed non-cell-based assay to evaluate drug permeability across the cellular membrane.
Question: Given a drug SMILES string, predict whether it
(A) is not permeable to cell membranes in a PAMPA assay (B) is permeable to cell membranes in a PAMPA assay
